\displaystyle\big\langle\tilde{m}_{E},\phi_{\ell,\nu}\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{\frac{\omega_{\nu}}{\zeta}}}=-a_{\ell}\|\phi_{\ell,\nu}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\frac{\omega_{\nu}}{\zeta}}}\left(\frac{\nu_{\tau}}{\nu}\frac{1}{\ln\nu}+\frac{\beta_{\tau}}{\beta}\ell\right)+O(|\ln\nu|^{\frac{1}{2}}),

(252)

\displaystyle\big\langle\tilde{m}_{E},\phi_{0,\nu}\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{\frac{\omega_{\nu}}{\zeta}}}\sim-a_{\ell}\frac{\nu_{\tau}}{8\nu}+O(|\ln\nu|^{-\frac{1}{2}}),

(253)

and

\sum_{n=1,n\neq\ell}^{N}\Big|\big\langle\tilde{m}_{E},\phi_{n,\nu}\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{\frac{\omega_{\nu}}{\zeta}}}\Big|\lesssim|\ln\nu|\nu^{2},\quad\|\tilde{m}_{E}\|_{L^{2}_{\frac{\omega_{\nu}}{\zeta}}}\lesssim\nu^{2}.

(254)

Proof.

Since the proof is exactly the same to that of Lemma
3.1
, we only sketch it. From the a priori bounds, we focus on the leading order term of
\tilde{m}_{E}
which is

\tilde{m}_{E}\sim-a_{\ell}\left(\frac{\nu_{\tau}}{\nu}\nu\partial_{\nu}(\phi_{\ell,\nu}-\phi_{0,\nu})+\frac{\beta_{\tau}}{\beta}\beta\partial_{\beta}(\phi_{\ell,\nu}-\phi_{0,\nu})\right).

(255)

We introduce (the logarithmic cancellation as
r\to\infty
is a consequence of (
33
)):

\Theta_{j}=(2j-2)T_{j}-r\partial_{r}T_{j}=_{r\to\infty}\left\{\begin{array}[]{l l}-\hat{d}_{j}r^{2j-2}+O(r^{2j-4}\ln r)\qquad\mbox{if }j\geq 1,\\
O(r^{-4})\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad\mbox{if }j=0,\end{array}\right.

(256)

with
\hat{d}_{1}=-\frac{1}{2}
,
\hat{d}_{j}=-\frac{\hat{d}_{j-1}}{4j(j-1)}
. This and (
61
) imply the identities:

\displaystyle\nu\partial_{\nu}(\phi_{\ell,\nu}-\phi_{0,\nu})=\sum_{j=1}^{\ell}c_{\ell,j}\beta^{j}\nu^{2j-2}\Theta_{j}(\zeta/\nu)+\nu\partial_{\nu}\tilde{\phi}_{\ell,\nu}-\nu\partial_{\nu}\tilde{\phi}_{0,\nu},

(257)

\displaystyle\beta\partial_{\beta}(\phi_{\ell,\nu}-\phi_{0,\nu})=\sum_{j=1}^{\ell}jc_{\ell,j}\beta^{j}\nu^{2j-2}T_{j}(\zeta/\nu)+\beta\partial_{\beta}\tilde{\phi}_{\ell,\nu}-\beta\partial_{\beta}\tilde{\phi}_{0,\nu}.

(258)

From Proposition 1 in
[
10
]
we have the following estimates for the error in (
61
):

\|D^{k}\tilde{\phi}_{j,\nu}\|_{L^{2}_{\frac{\omega_{\nu}}{\zeta}}}+\|D^{k}\nu\partial_{\nu}\tilde{\phi}_{j,\nu}\|_{L^{2}_{\frac{\omega_{\nu}}{\zeta}}}+\|D^{k}\beta\partial_{\beta}\tilde{\phi}_{j,\nu}\|_{L^{2}_{\frac{\omega_{\nu}}{\zeta}}}\lesssim|\ln\nu|^{-\frac{1}{2}}

(259)

for
k=0,1,2
and
0\leq j\leq N
. Thus, the a priori bounds (
250
), the identities (
257
) and (
258
), the asymptotics (
256
) and (
33
) and the bounds (
259
) prove the second bound in (
254
). This bound in turns imply the first one in (
254
) after applying Cauchy-Schhwarz and (
58
). We have proved (
254
) for the leading term (
255
).

Next, we write
\langle\cdot\rangle=\langle\cdot\rangle_{L^{2}_{\frac{\omega_{\nu}}{\zeta}}}
. The identities (
257
) and (
258
), the decomposition (
61
) and the bound (
259
) imply that, where we change variables
\zeta\mapsto r=\zeta/\nu
for the main term:

\displaystyle\langle\nu\partial_{\nu}(\phi_{\ell,\nu}-\phi_{0,\nu}),\phi_{0,\nu}\rangle=\sum_{j=1}^{\ell}c_{\ell,j}\beta^{j}\nu^{2j}\int_{0}^{\infty}\Theta_{j}(r)T_{0}(r)\frac{e^{-\beta\nu^{2}r^{2}/2}}{rU(r)}dr+O(|\ln\nu|^{-\frac{1}{2}}),

\displaystyle\langle\beta\partial_{\beta}(\phi_{\ell,\nu}-\phi_{0,\nu}),\phi_{0,\nu}\rangle=\sum_{j=1}^{\ell}jc_{\ell,j}\beta^{j}\nu^{2j}\int_{0}^{\infty}T_{j}(r)T_{0}(r)\frac{e^{-\beta\nu^{2}r^{2}/2}}{rU(r)}dr+O(|\ln\nu|^{\frac{1}{2}}).

We compute the numbers appearing above. By (
256
) and (
33
):

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{\ell}c_{\ell,j}\beta^{j}\nu^{2j}\int_{0}^{\infty}\Theta_{j}(r)T_{0}(r)\frac{e^{-\beta\nu^{2}r^{2}/2}}{rU(r)}dr=-\sum_{j=1}^{\ell}c_{\ell,j}\hat{d}_{j}2^{j-4}\int_{0}^{\infty}\xi^{j-1}e^{-\xi}d\xi+O(\nu)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{8}\sum_{j=1}^{\ell}(-1)^{j+1}\frac{\ell!}{(\ell-j)!j!}\ =\ \frac{1}{8}+O(\nu),

using that
\hat{d}_{j}=(-1)^{j}\frac{2^{-2j+1}}{j[(j-1)!]^{2}}
and
c_{\ell,j}=2^{j}\frac{\ell!}{(\ell-j)!}
from (
33
) and (
62
). Hence

\langle\nu\partial_{\nu}(\phi_{\ell,\nu}-\phi_{0,\nu}),\phi_{0,\nu}\rangle=\frac{1}{8}+O(|\ln\nu|^{-\frac{1}{2}}).

(260)

Similarly, using (
33
) and the identity
\int_{0}^{\infty}\xi^{j-1}e^{-\xi}d\xi=(j-1)!
:

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{\ell}jc_{\ell,j}\beta^{j}\nu^{2j}\int_{0}^{\infty}T_{j}(r)T_{0}(r)\frac{e^{-\beta\nu^{2}r^{2}/2}}{rU(r)}dr=|\ln\nu|\sum_{j=1}^{\ell}c_{\ell,j}\hat{d}_{j}2^{j-4}j!+O(1)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{8}|\ln\nu|\sum_{j=1}^{\ell}(-1)^{j}\frac{\ell!}{(\ell-j)!(j-1)!}+O(1)\ =\ O(1).

Hence

\langle\beta\partial_{\beta}(\phi_{\ell,\nu}-\phi_{0,\nu}),\phi_{0,\nu}\rangle=O(|\ln\nu|^{\frac{1}{2}}).

(261)

Next, we recall that
\|\phi_{\ell,\nu}\|_{L^{2}_{\frac{\omega_{\nu}}{\zeta}}}=c_{\ell}\ln^{2}\nu
from (
58
). Differentiating, we get:

2\langle\nu\partial_{\nu}\phi_{\ell,\nu},\phi_{\ell,\nu}\rangle=2c_{\ell}\ln\nu-\langle\phi_{\ell,\nu},\phi_{\ell,\nu}\frac{\nu\partial_{\nu}\omega_{\nu}}{\omega_{\nu}}\rangle=2c_{\ell}\ln\nu+O(|\ln\nu|^{\frac{1}{2}})

where we used the cancellation
|\nu\partial_{\nu}\omega_{\nu}|\lesssim\omega_{\nu}\langle\zeta/\nu\rangle^{-2}
, (
33
) and (
259
). We recall the orthogonality
\langle\phi_{\ell,\nu},\phi_{0,\nu}\rangle=0
. Differentiating it, using the same cancellation, (
33
) and (
259
) we get:

2\langle\nu\partial_{\nu}\phi_{0,\nu},\phi_{\ell,\nu}\rangle=-\langle\phi_{0,\nu},\phi_{\ell,\nu}\frac{\nu\partial_{\nu}\omega_{\nu}}{\omega_{\nu}}\rangle=O(|\ln\nu|^{\frac{1}{2}}).

Collecting the two estimates above we have proved:

\langle\nu\partial_{\nu}(\phi_{\ell,\nu}-\phi_{0,\nu}),\phi_{\ell,\nu}\rangle=c_{\ell}\ln\nu+O(|\ln\nu|^{\frac{1}{2}}).

(262)

Next, we write from (
256
) and (
23
), using that
\mathcal{A}_{0}T_{j}=-T_{j-1}
:

jT_{j}=T_{j}+\frac{1}{2}r\partial_{r}T_{j}+\frac{1}{2}\Theta_{j}=T_{j}-(2\beta\nu^{2})^{-1}\mathcal{A}T_{j}-(2\beta\nu^{2})^{-1}T_{j-1}+\frac{1}{2}\Theta_{j}.

Above, we recall that from (
61
) and (
259
), for
j<n
one has
\nu^{2j-2}T_{j}(\zeta/\nu)=\sum_{0}^{j}\tilde{c}_{j,i}\phi_{i,\nu}+O_{L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}/\zeta}}(|\ln\nu|^{1/2})
for some constants
\tilde{c}_{j,i}
. Using this, the above identity, the identity (
61
), the fact that
\mathcal{A}^{\zeta}\phi_{\ell,\nu}=\alpha_{\ell,\nu}\phi_{\ell,\nu}
and (
56
) we obtain:

\beta\partial_{\beta}\phi_{\ell,\nu}=\ell\phi_{\ell,\nu}+\sum_{j<n}\tilde{c}_{j}\phi_{j,\nu}+O_{L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}/\zeta}}(|\ln\nu|^{1/2})

for some constants
\tilde{c}_{j}
. Therefore, from the orthogonality of the eigenfunctions:

\langle\beta\partial_{\beta}\phi_{\ell,\nu},\phi_{\ell,\nu}\rangle=\ell\|\phi_{\ell,\nu}\|_{L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}/\zeta}}^{2}+O(|\ln\nu|^{3/2}).

Since from (
61
),
\partial_{\beta}\phi_{0,\nu}=\partial_{\beta}\tilde{\phi}_{0,\nu}
, from (
58
), (
259
) and Cauchy-Schwarz we get
\langle\beta\partial_{\beta}\phi_{0,\nu},\phi_{\ell,\nu}\rangle=O(|\ln\nu|^{3/2})
. This and the above give

\langle\beta\partial_{\beta}(\phi_{\ell,\nu}-\phi_{0,\nu}),\phi_{\ell,\nu}\rangle=\ell\|\phi_{\ell,\nu}\|_{L^{2}_{\omega_{\nu}/\zeta}}^{2}+O(|\ln\nu|^{3/2}).

(263)

Collecting (
260
), (
261
), (
262
) and (
263
), using the a priori bounds (
250
), we have established (
252
) and (
253
) for the main order term (
255
).

∎

Remark 5.2

(Unstable blowup rates)
.

From Lemma
5.1
, we project (
251
) onto
\phi_{0,\nu}
and
\phi_{\ell,\nu}
to obtain the following system of ODEs

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l l}&\left(\frac{\nu_{\tau}}{\nu}-\beta\right)8\nu^{2}+a_{\ell,\tau}-2\beta a_{\ell}\big(1+\frac{1}{2\ln\nu}\big)+a_{\ell}\frac{\nu_{\tau}}{\nu}\frac{1}{\ln\nu}=\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\nu^{2}}{|\ln\nu|^{3/2}}\right),\\
&a_{\ell,\tau}-2\beta a_{\ell}\big(1-\ell+\frac{1}{2\ln\nu}\big)+a_{\ell}\frac{\nu_{\tau}}{\nu}\frac{1}{\ln\nu}+\ell a_{\ell}\frac{\beta_{\tau}}{\beta}=\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\nu^{2}}{|\ln\nu|^{3/2}}\right).\end{array}\right.

(264)

We solve this system for
0<\nu\ll 1
and
\beta\approx 1
under the compatibility condition

\frac{a_{\ell}}{4\nu^{2}}=-1+\frac{1}{2\ln\nu},

(265)

which is a constraint on
\beta
. Namely one obtains from (
264
) that this condition is satisfied provided that
\beta_{\tau}=O(|\ln\nu|^{-3/2})
. Under (
265
), (
264
) gives:

\frac{\nu_{\tau}}{\nu}=\beta(1-\ell)+\frac{\beta\ell}{2\ln\nu}+O(|\ln\nu|^{-\frac{3}{2}}).

Solving this yields that
\beta\to\beta_{\infty}
and
\nu(\tau)\sim e^{\beta(1-\ell)\tau}\tau^{\frac{\ell}{2(1-\ell)}}\nu_{\infty}
for some
\beta_{\infty},\nu_{\infty}>0
. Since
\mu_{\tau}=\beta\mu
,
\partial_{t}\tau=\mu^{-2}
and
\lambda=\mu\nu
, we get that for some blow up time
T>0
:

\lambda(t)\sim C(u_{0})(T-t)^{\frac{\ell}{2}}|\ln(T-t)|^{-\frac{\ell}{2(\ell-1)}}.

(266)

Remark 5.3
.

Note that
\int|\ln\nu|^{-\alpha}d\tau<\infty
for all
\alpha>1
, which is not the case for the stable blow-up law (
120
) where
\alpha>2
is needed. This is of a simplification for the instable case: one can only perform the analysis with an accuracy of one order in
|\ln\nu|^{-1}
less than for the stable case and still be able to close the estimates.

Bootstrap regime.

Lemma
5.1
provides information about the size of the error and Remark
5.2
formally gives us the law of
\nu
, from which we can redesign the bootstrap regime (
3.3
) adapted to the case
\ell\geq 2
. In particular, we control the remainder
\varepsilon
according to the following regime.

Definition 5.4

(Bootstrap)
.

Let
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
with
\ell\geq 2
and
\tau_{0}\gg 1
. A solution
w
is said to be trapped on
[\tau_{0},\tau^{*}]
if it satisfies the initial bootstrap conditions in the sense of Definition
3.2
at time
\tau_{0}
and the following conditions on
(\tau_{0},\tau^{*}]
. There exists
\mu\in C^{1}([0,t^{*}],(0,\infty))
and constants
K^{\prime\prime}\gg K^{\prime}\gg K\gg 1
such that the solution can be decomposed according to (
88
), (
89
) on
(\tau_{0},\tau^{*}]
with:

(i)

(Compatibility condition for the renormalisation rate
\beta
)

\frac{a_{\ell}}{4\nu^{2}}=-1+\frac{1}{2\ln\nu},

(ii)

(Modulation parameters)

e^{\frac{(1-\ell)\tau}{2}}\tau^{\frac{\ell}{2(1-\ell)}}\leq\nu(\tau)\leq 2e^{\frac{(1-\ell)\tau}{2}}\tau^{\frac{\ell}{2(1-\ell)}}\ \ \mbox{ and }\ \ \frac{1}{2}<\beta<2,

|a_{n}|<\frac{\nu^{2}}{|\ln\nu|}\ \mbox{ for }1\leq n\neq\ell\leq N.

(iii)

(Remainder)

\|m_{\varepsilon}(\tau)\|_{L^{2}_{\frac{\omega_{\nu}}{\zeta}}}<K\nu^{2},\quad\|m_{\varepsilon}(\tau)\|_{H^{2}(\zeta_{*}\leq\zeta\leq\zeta^{*})}<K^{\prime}\nu^{2},

\quad\|\tilde{m}_{v}\|_{\textup{in}}<K^{\prime\prime}\nu^{2},\quad\|\hat{w}^{0}\|_{\textup{ex}}<K^{\prime\prime}\nu^{2}.

The bootstrap definition
5.4
is almost the same as the one defined in Definition
3.3
, except for the bounds on
m_{\varepsilon}
which are of size
\nu^{2}
only, see remark
5.3
. All the energy estimates as well as the derivation of the modulation equations given in Section
4
can be adapted to the new definition
5.4
without any difficulties to derive the conclusion of Theorem
1.3
.

Appendix A
Some useful estimates

Hardy-Poincaré type inequality:
Let us recall the following estimates in spaces with weights involving
\rho
, for functions
v
and
u
without radial components. The first one is a Poincaré-type inequality

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}v^{2}|z|^{2k}(1+|z|^{2})e^{-\frac{z^{2}}{2}}dz\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}|\nabla v|^{2}|z|^{2k}e^{-\frac{z^{2}}{2}}dz,

(267)

for any
k\geq 0
. The second one is a Hardy type inequality: for
0<b<1
, there exists
C>0
independent of
b
such that

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}(1+|y|^{2})u^{2}e^{-\frac{b|y|^{2}}{2}}\leq C\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}(1+|y|^{4})|\nabla u|^{2}e^{-\frac{b|y|^{2}}{2}}.

(268)

By the change of variables
z=\sqrt{b}y
, the two above inequalities imply for any
1\leq\alpha\leq 3
:

b^{\alpha-1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}|q^{\perp}|^{2}(1+|y|^{2\alpha})\rho dy\leq C_{\alpha}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\frac{|\nabla q^{\perp}|^{2}}{U}\rho dy.

(269)

Estimates on the Poison field:
For
u
localized on a single spherical harmonics, the Laplace operator is written as

\Delta u(x)=\Delta^{(k)}(u^{(k,i)})(r)\phi^{(k,i)}(\theta),\ \ \Delta^{(k)}:=\partial_{rr}+\frac{1}{r}\partial_{r}-\frac{k^{2}}{r^{2}}.

The fundamental solutions to
\Delta^{(k)}f=0
are

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l l}\log(r)\ \ \text{and}\ \ 1\ \ \text{for}\ k=0,\\
r^{k}\ \ \text{and}\ \ r^{-k}\ \text{for}\ k\geq 1.\end{array}\right.

and their Wronskian relation

W^{(0)}=\frac{d}{dr}\log(r)=r^{-1}\ \ \text{and}\ \ W^{(k)}=\frac{d}{dr}(r^{k})r^{-k}-r^{k}\frac{d}{dr}(r^{-k})=2kr^{-1}\ \text{for}\ k\geq 1.

The solution to the Laplace equation
-\Delta\Phi_{u}=u
given by
\Phi_{u}=-(2\pi)^{-1}\log(|x|)*u
is given on spherical harmonics by:

\Phi_{u}^{(0,0)}(r)=-\log(r)\int_{0}^{r}u^{(0,0)}(\tilde{r})\tilde{r}d\tilde{r}-\int_{r}^{+\infty}u^{(0,0)}(\tilde{r})\log(\tilde{r})\tilde{r}d\tilde{r},

\nabla\Phi_{u}^{(0,0)}(x)=-\frac{x}{|x|^{2}}\int_{0}^{|x|}u^{(0,0)}(\tilde{r})\tilde{r}d\tilde{r},

\Phi_{u}^{(k,i)}(r)=\frac{r^{k}}{2k}\int_{r}^{+\infty}u^{(k,i)}(\tilde{r})\tilde{r}^{1-k}d\tilde{r}+\frac{r^{-k}}{2k}\int_{0}^{r}u^{(k,i)}(\tilde{r})\tilde{r}^{1+k}d\tilde{r},

(270)

\partial_{r}\Phi_{u}^{(k,i)}(r)=\frac{r^{k-1}}{2}\int_{r}^{+\infty}u^{(k,i)}(\tilde{r})\tilde{r}^{1-k}d\tilde{r}-\frac{r^{-k-1}}{2}\int_{0}^{r}u^{(k,i)}(\tilde{r})\tilde{r}^{1+k}d\tilde{r},

(271)

The following lemma gives pointwise estimates of the Poison field.

Lemma A.1
.

If
u^{(0,0)}=0
, there holds the estimate for any
\alpha>0
,

|\Phi_{u}|^{2}+|y|^{2}|\nabla\Phi_{u}|^{2}\lesssim|y|^{2}(1+|y|)^{-2\alpha}\big(1+\mathbf{1}_{|y|\leq 1}|\log|y||\big)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}|u|^{2}(1+|y|)^{2\alpha}dy.

(272)

Proof.

See Lemma A.1 in
[
10
]
.
∎

For the control of the outer part, we need the following estimates for the Poison field in term of the outer norm.

Lemma A.2
.

For
|z|\geq 1
one has the estimate,

\frac{1}{\zeta}|\Phi_{(1-\chi_{\frac{\zeta^{*}}{2}})\hat{w}}(z)|+|\nabla\Phi_{(1-\chi_{\frac{\zeta^{*}}{2}})\hat{w}}(z)|+\zeta|\nabla^{2}\Phi_{(1-\chi_{\frac{\zeta^{*}}{2}})\hat{w}}(z)|\lesssim\frac{1}{\zeta^{\frac{1}{2}}}\|\hat{w}\|_{\textup{ex}}.

(273)

Proof.

Notice that for
v(y)=\nu^{2}w(z)
, the Poisson field scales in
L^{\infty}
, that is:
\Phi_{v}(y)=\Phi_{w}(z)
. Hence (
272
) holds also in
z
variables. We apply it with
\alpha=\frac{1}{2}
, so that for
|z|\geq 1
:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|z|}|\Phi_{(1-\chi_{\frac{\zeta^{*}}{2}})\hat{w}}|^{2}+|z||\nabla\Phi_{(1-\chi_{\frac{\zeta^{*}}{2}})\hat{w}}|^{2}\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}|(1-\chi_{\frac{\zeta^{*}}{2}})\hat{w}|^{2}|z|dz\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}(1-\chi_{\frac{\zeta^{*}}{2}})\hat{w}^{2}|z|^{4-\frac{1}{2}}|z|^{-\frac{1}{2}}\frac{dz}{|z|^{2}}

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}(1-\chi_{\frac{\zeta^{*}}{2}})|\hat{w}|z|^{2-\frac{1}{4}}|^{2p}\frac{dz}{|z|^{2}}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}(1-\chi_{\frac{\zeta^{*}}{2}})|z|^{-\frac{1}{2}p^{\prime}}\frac{dz}{|z|^{2}}\right)^{\frac{1}{p^{\prime}}}\lesssim\|\hat{w}\|_{\textup{ex}}^{2}

where we used
(1-\chi_{\frac{\zeta^{*}}{2}})\leq 1
and Hölder with
p^{\prime}
the conjugate exponent of
p
. Next, we decompose
\hat{w}=\hat{w}^{0}+\hat{w}^{\perp}
between radial and non radial components. One has
\|\hat{w}^{\perp}\|_{\textup{ex}}+\|\hat{w}^{0}\|_{\textup{ex}}\lesssim\|\hat{w}\|_{\textup{ex}}
. from the Sobolev embedding of
W^{3/p,2p}
into the Hölder space
C^{1/p}
in dimension
2
, we obtain by interpolation that for any
|z|\geq 1
:

\displaystyle\|(1-\chi_{\frac{\zeta^{*}}{2}})\hat{w}^{\perp}\|_{C^{\frac{1}{p}}(B(z,\frac{|z|}{2}))}\leq C\|\hat{w}^{\perp}\|_{W^{\frac{3}{p},2p}(B(z,\frac{|z|}{2}))}\leq C\|\hat{w}^{\perp}\|_{L^{2p}(B(z,\frac{|z|}{2}))}^{1-\frac{3}{p}}\|\nabla\hat{w}^{\perp}\|_{L^{2p}(B(z,\frac{|z|}{2}))}^{\frac{3}{p}}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{C}{\zeta^{\frac{3}{2}}}\|\zeta^{2-\frac{1}{2}}\hat{w}^{\perp}\|_{L^{2p}(B(z,\frac{|z|}{2}))}^{1-\frac{3}{p}}\|\zeta^{2-\frac{1}{2}}\nabla\hat{w}^{\perp}\|_{L^{2p}(B(z,\frac{|z|}{2}))}^{\frac{3}{p}}\leq\frac{C}{\zeta^{\frac{3}{2}}}\|\hat{w}\|_{\textup{ex}},

where we used the definition of the
\|\hat{w}\|_{\textup{ex}}
norm and the inequality
1/2\geq 1/4+1/p
for
p
large enough. Therefore, in the ball
B(z,|z|/2)
,
\Phi_{(1-\chi_{\frac{\zeta^{*}}{2}})\hat{w}^{\perp}}
solves
-\Delta\Phi_{(1-\chi_{\frac{\zeta^{*}}{2}})\hat{w}^{\perp}}=(1-\chi_{\frac{\zeta^{*}}{2}})\hat{w}^{\perp}
with the estimates:

\frac{1}{|z|^{\frac{1}{2}}}|\Phi_{(1-\chi_{\frac{\zeta^{*}}{2}})\hat{w}^{\perp}}|+|z|^{\frac{1}{2}}|\nabla\Phi_{(1-\chi_{\frac{\zeta^{*}}{2}})\hat{w}^{\perp}}+|z|^{\frac{3}{2}}\|(1-\chi_{\frac{\zeta^{*}}{2}})\hat{w}^{\perp}\|_{C^{1/p}(B(z,|z|/2)}\leq C\|\hat{w}\|_{\textup{ex}}.

By standard regularity properties of the Dirichlet problem and a rescaling argument, we obtain that
\|\nabla^{2}\Phi_{(1-\chi_{\frac{\zeta^{*}}{2}})\hat{w}^{\perp}}\|_{B(z,\frac{|z|}{2}}\leq|z|^{-3/2}C\|\hat{w}\|_{\textup{ex}}
. This proves (
273
) for the nonradial part of
\hat{w}
. Next, for the radial part, we have for
i=1,2
and
\zeta\geq 1
:

\displaystyle\nabla\partial_{z_{i}}\Phi_{(1-\chi_{\frac{\zeta^{*}}{2}})\hat{w}^{0}}(z)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\nabla\left(\frac{z_{i}}{\zeta^{2}}\int_{0}^{\zeta}(1-\chi_{\frac{\zeta^{*}}{2}})\hat{w}^{0}(\tilde{\zeta})\tilde{\zeta}d\tilde{\zeta}\right),

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\nabla(z_{i})\frac{1}{\zeta^{2}}\int_{0}^{\zeta}(1-\chi_{\frac{\zeta^{*}}{2}})\hat{w}^{0}(\tilde{\zeta})\tilde{\zeta}d\tilde{\zeta}+\frac{2z_{i}z}{\zeta^{4}}\int_{0}^{\zeta}(1-\chi_{\frac{\zeta^{*}}{2}})\hat{w}^{0}(\tilde{\zeta})\tilde{\zeta}d\tilde{\zeta}-\frac{2z_{i}z}{\zeta^{2}}(1-\chi_{\frac{\zeta^{*}}{2}})\hat{w}^{0}(\zeta).

From Hölder, where
(2p)^{\prime}
is the Hölder conjugate of
2p
:

\displaystyle\left|\int_{0}^{\zeta}(1-\chi_{\frac{\zeta^{*}}{2}})\hat{w}^{0}(\tilde{\zeta})\tilde{\zeta}d\tilde{\zeta}\right|=\left|\int_{0}^{\zeta}(1-\chi_{\frac{\zeta^{*}}{2}})\tilde{\zeta}^{2-\frac{1}{4}-\frac{1}{p}}\hat{w}^{0}(\tilde{\zeta})\tilde{\zeta}^{-1+\frac{1}{4}+\frac{1}{p}}d\tilde{\zeta}\right|\lesssim\left|\int_{0}^{\zeta}(1-\chi_{\frac{\zeta^{*}}{2}})\tilde{\zeta}^{2-\frac{1}{4}-\frac{1}{2p}}\hat{w}^{0}(\tilde{\zeta})\tilde{\zeta}^{-1+\frac{1}{4}+\frac{1}{2p}}d\tilde{\zeta}\right|

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\left(\int_{0}^{\zeta}(1-\chi_{\frac{\zeta^{*}}{2}})|\tilde{\zeta}^{2-\frac{1}{4}}\hat{w}^{0}(\tilde{\zeta})|\frac{d\tilde{\zeta}}{\zeta}\right)^{\frac{1}{2p}}\left(\int_{0}^{\zeta}(1-\chi_{\frac{\zeta^{*}}{2}})\tilde{\zeta}^{(-1+\frac{1}{4}+\frac{1}{2p})(2p)^{\prime}}d\tilde{\zeta}\right)^{\frac{1}{(2p)^{\prime}}}\lesssim\|\hat{w}\|_{\textup{ex}}\zeta^{\frac{1}{4}}.

We recall that
|\hat{w}^{0}|\lesssim\zeta^{-3/2}
for
\zeta\geq 1
from (
140
). This, and the two above identities imply that for
\zeta\geq 1
:

\left|\nabla\partial_{z_{i}}\Phi_{(1-\chi_{\frac{\zeta^{*}}{2}})\hat{w}^{0}}(z)\right|\lesssim\zeta^{-\frac{3}{2}}\|\hat{w}\|_{\textup{ex}}.

This proves (
273
) for the radial part of
\hat{w}
.

∎

Appendix B
Coercivity of
\mathscr{A}_{0}

In this section, we aim at deriving the coercive estimate of
\mathscr{A}_{0}
which is the key to establish the monotonicity formula of the inner norm (
125
). We first claim the following.

Lemma B.1

(Coercivity of
\mathscr{A}_{0}
)
.

Assume that
f:[0,\infty)\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
satisfies

\int_{0}^{\infty}\left(\frac{|f|^{2}}{1+r^{2}}+|\partial_{r}f|^{2}\right)\frac{\omega_{0}(r)}{r}dr<\infty,

(274)

and

\int_{0}^{\infty}f(r)T_{0}(r)\chi_{{}_{M}}(r)\frac{\omega_{0}}{r}dr=0.

Then, there exists a constant
\delta_{0}>0
such that

\int_{0}^{\infty}f\mathscr{A}_{0}f\frac{\omega_{0}}{r}dr\leq-\delta_{0}\int_{0}^{\infty}\left(\frac{|f|^{2}}{1+r^{2}}+|\partial_{r}f|^{2}\right)\frac{\omega_{0}}{r}dr.

Proof.

Step 1
We first claim the Hardy inequality for any
f
satisfying (
274
):

\int_{0}^{\infty}|\partial_{r}f|^{2}\frac{\omega_{0}}{r}dr\gtrsim\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{f^{2}}{1+r^{2}}\frac{\omega_{0}}{r}dr.

(275)

We first study the function near the origin. If
f
satisfies (
274
) then by standard one dimensional Sobolev embedding,
f
is continuous on
[0,1]
. Hence the estimate (
274
) implies
f(0)=0
. From the fundamental Theorem of Calculus (which is justified for
f
via a standard approximation procedure):

\displaystyle\left|f(r)\right|

\displaystyle=\left|f(0)+\int_{0}^{r}\partial_{r}(f)d\tilde{r}\right|=\left|\int_{0}^{r}\partial_{r}(f)d\tilde{r}\right|\leq\left(\int_{0}^{r}\frac{|\partial_{r}(f)|^{2}}{\tilde{r}}d\tilde{r}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\int_{0}^{r}\tilde{r}d\tilde{r}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\lesssim r\int_{0}^{\infty}|\partial_{r}f|^{2}\frac{\omega_{0}}{\tilde{r}}d\tilde{r}.

This proves that:

\int_{0}^{\infty}|\partial_{r}f|^{2}\frac{\omega_{0}}{r}dr\gtrsim\int_{0}^{1}\frac{f^{2}}{1+r^{2}}\frac{\omega_{0}}{r}dr.

(276)

Away from the origin, integrating by parts:

-\int_{1}^{\infty}\partial_{r}ffr^{2}dr=\int_{1}^{\infty}f^{2}rdr+f^{2}(1).

By Cauchy-Schwarz and Hardy:

\left|\int_{1}^{\infty}\partial_{r}ffr^{2}dr\right|\leq\frac{1}{2}\int_{1}^{\infty}|\partial_{r}f|^{2}r^{3}dr+\frac{1}{2}\int_{1}^{\infty}f^{2}rdr.

The two above identities then give:

\displaystyle\int_{1}^{\infty}f^{2}rdr

\displaystyle\leq-\int_{1}^{\infty}\partial_{r}ffr^{2}dr-f^{2}(1)\leq-\int_{1}^{\infty}\partial_{r}ffr^{2}dr\leq\frac{1}{2}\int_{1}^{\infty}|\partial_{r}f|^{2}r^{3}dr+\frac{1}{2}\int_{1}^{\infty}f^{2}rdr,

from what one deduces, since for
r\geq 1
, one has
cr^{4}\leq\omega_{0}(r)\leq r^{4}/c
for some
c>0
:

\int_{1}^{\infty}f^{2}rdr\leq\int_{1}^{\infty}|\partial_{r}f|^{2}r^{3}dr\lesssim\int_{0}^{\infty}|\partial_{r}f|^{2}\frac{\omega_{0}}{r}dr.

(277)

The two estimates (
276
) and (
277
) imply the desired Hardy inequality (
275
).

Step 2

Proof of the coercivity estimate
.
\mathscr{A}_{0}
has
T_{0}(r)
in its kernel, with
T_{0}
a strictly positive function on
(0,\infty)
implies that the spectrum of this self-adjoint operator is nonnegative by standard Sturm-Liouville argument. Hence for any
f
satisfying (
274
):

\int_{0}^{\infty}f\mathscr{A}_{0}f\frac{\omega_{0}}{r}dr\leq 0.

Integrating by parts:

\int_{0}^{\infty}f\mathscr{A}_{0}f\frac{\omega_{0}}{r}dr=-\int_{0}^{\infty}|\partial_{r}f|^{2}\frac{\omega_{0}}{r}dr+\int_{0}^{\infty}f^{2}\frac{dr}{r}.

(278)

Combining this and Step 1 gives that for some
c,C>0
, for any
f
satisfying (
274
):

\int_{0}^{\infty}f\mathscr{A}_{0}f\frac{\omega_{0}}{r}dr\leq-c\int_{0}^{\infty}\left(\frac{|f|^{2}}{1+r^{2}}+|\partial_{r}f|^{2}\right)\frac{\omega_{0}}{r}dr+C\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{f^{2}}{r}dr.

(279)

We now assume by contradiction that there exists a sequence of functions
f_{n}
with

\int_{0}^{\infty}\left(\frac{|f_{n}|^{2}}{1+r^{2}}+|\partial_{r}f_{n}|^{2}\right)\frac{\omega_{0}(r)}{r}dr=1,

(280)

and

\int_{0}^{\infty}f_{n}\mathscr{A}_{0}f_{n}\frac{\omega_{0}}{r}dr\rightarrow 0,\ \ \int_{0}^{\infty}f_{n}(r)\chi(r)T_{0}(r)dr=0.

Up to a subsequence,
f_{n}
converges weakly in
H^{1}_{loc}
and strongly in
L^{2}_{loc}
to some function
f_{\infty}
. By lower-semicontinuity of the above norm, and by strong continuity in
L^{2}_{loc}
it satisfies:

\int_{0}^{\infty}\left(\frac{|f_{\infty}|^{2}}{1+r^{2}}+|\partial_{r}f_{\infty}|^{2}\right)\frac{\omega_{0}(r)}{r}dr\leq 1,\ \ \int_{0}^{\infty}f_{\infty}(r)\chi(r)T_{0}(r)dr=0.

From the bound
\int_{0}^{\infty}|f_{\infty}|^{2}\omega_{0}/(r+r^{3})dr\leq 1
and the strong continuity in
L^{2}_{loc}
one has:

\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{f_{n}^{2}}{r}dr\rightarrow\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{f_{\infty}^{2}}{r}dr.

The subcoercivity (
279
) and the first bound in (
280
) imply
\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{f_{n}^{2}}{r}dr\geq c
for some
c>0
. From the above strong convergence this implies:

\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{f_{\infty}^{2}}{r}dr\geq c>0.

From (
278
), (
280
), the aforementioned strong convergence and lower semi-continuity:

\displaystyle 0\leq-\int_{0}^{\infty}f\mathscr{A}_{0}f\frac{\omega_{0}}{r}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}|\partial_{r}f|^{2}\frac{\omega_{0}}{r}dr-\int_{0}^{\infty}f^{2}\frac{dr}{r}\leq\liminf\left(\int_{0}^{\infty}|\partial_{r}f_{n}|^{2}\frac{\omega_{0}}{r}dr-\int_{0}^{\infty}f_{n}^{2}\frac{dr}{r}\right)=0.

Hence
\int_{0}^{\infty}f\mathscr{A}_{0}f\frac{\omega_{0}}{r}=0
. Hence
f_{\infty}=\lambda T_{0}(r)
, for some
\lambda\neq 0
, which contradicts with
\int_{0}^{\infty}f_{\infty}\chi T_{0}=0
. This concludes the proof of Lemma
B.1
.
∎

We also have the following coercivity estimate for
\mathscr{A}_{0}
.

Lemma B.2

(Coercivity of
\mathscr{A}_{0}
)
.

Assume that
f:[0,\infty)\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
satisfies

\int_{0}^{\infty}\left(|\partial^{2}_{r}f|^{2}+\frac{|\partial_{r}f|^{2}}{1+r^{2}}+\frac{|f|^{2}}{(1+r^{4})(1+\ln^{2}\langle r\rangle)}\right)\frac{\omega_{0}(r)}{r}dr<\infty,

and

\int_{0}^{\infty}f(r)T_{0}(r)\chi_{{}_{M}}(r)\frac{\omega_{0}}{r}dr=0.

Then, there exists a constant
\delta_{1}>0
such that

\int_{0}^{\infty}|\mathscr{A}_{0}f|^{2}\frac{\omega_{0}}{r}dr\geq\delta_{1}\int_{0}^{\infty}\left(|\partial^{2}_{r}f|^{2}+\frac{|\partial_{r}f|^{2}}{1+r^{2}}+\frac{|f|^{2}}{(1+r^{4})(1+\ln^{2}\langle r\rangle)}\right)\frac{\omega_{0}(r)}{r}dr.

Proof.

Since the proof follows exactly the same lines to the one of Lemma
B.1
, apart from the following Hardy inequality (in the critical case) which is used to obtain a sub-coercivity estimate,

\int_{0}^{+\infty}\frac{|\partial_{r}f|^{2}}{1+r^{2}}\frac{\omega_{0}}{r}dr\geq C\int_{0}^{+\infty}\frac{|f|^{2}}{(1+r^{4})(1+\ln^{2}\langle r\rangle)}\frac{\omega_{0}}{r}dr.

(281)

so we omit the proof.
∎
